> QWEN ANSWER:  
> Based on the data I found, here are the key facts about military service in America:
  
> 
  
> ## **Military Service by Generation:**
  
> 
  
> - **Currently serving**: Only **~1%** of the U.S. population is actively serving in the military [[15]]
  
> - **Veterans**: About **7.3-7.4%** of all living Americans have ever served in the military [[11]][[12]]
  
> - **Vietnam generation**: About **9.7%** of that generation served [[52]][[54]]
  
> - The veteran population is **declining** as older generations pass away [[15]][[18]]
  
> 
  
> ## **Youth Interest in Military Service:**
  
> 
  
> This is where it gets striking:
  
> 
  
> - **Only 9-11%** of young people (ages 17-24) show any propensity to serve - this is at **historic lows** [[23]][[62]][[63]]
  
> - **71-77%** of youth aged 17-24 **don't even qualify** for military service due to obesity, drug use, or mental/physical health issues [[19]][[20]][[26]]
  
> - **50%** of youth admit they know "little to nothing" about military service [[21]][[40]]
  
> - **79%** of those who DO join have a relative who served - showing it's increasingly a "family tradition" rather than broad-based appeal [[15]]
  
> 
  
> ## **Public Opinion on War:**
  
> 
  
> - **Anti-war sentiment is surging** in the U.S., with economic concerns driving war fatigue [[45]]
  
> - There's a long-standing tension in American politics between interventionism and isolationism [[72]][[73]]
  
> 
  
> **Your observation appears correct**: Very few Americans actually serve, youth interest is at record lows, and there's growing war fatigue among the public - yet the document you've shown continues to emphasize military strength and readiness. This suggests a significant gap between the establishment's worldview and public sentiment.

[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Ts-AA6LQf6I&lc=UgxMa5jfxOiJkGoxBA54AaABAg)

A more plausible explanation for the widespread use of oil lies in a global shift in transportation infrastructure—one that favors systems more easily monitored and controlled. Historically, this transition can be compared to the decline of earlier technologies such as airships, which once traversed vast distances and symbolized a different vision of global travel. Their era, along with that of large ocean liners like the Titanic, has become partially obscured over time.

Both of these transportation modes eventually disappeared, replaced by oil-dependent systems that reshaped the global economy. Since then, the petroleum industry has held a dominant position in shaping modern infrastructure and power structures. Looking ahead, a similar transformation may occur again—this time toward an electrified system, supported by data-driven technologies and large-scale digital oversight.

Conspiracy Theory Channel
Increased Anxiety and Mistrust: These narratives often thrive on the idea that the world is a dangerous place managed by "hidden hands." Regularly engaging with this worldview can heighten feelings of anxiety, powerlessness, and a generalized mistrust of institutions and fellow citizens, even when there is no evidence for such fears.


The "Conjunction Fallacy": Conspiracy content often encourages the "conjunction fallacy"—the tendency to overestimate the probability of multiple complex, unrelated events happening together as part of a single, secret plot. This makes the world feel more chaotic and threatening than it actually is.

Reduced Cognitive Flexibility: These narratives often offer "black and white" explanations for complex geopolitical issues. By replacing nuanced reality with a simplistic story of "good vs. evil" or "the public vs. a secret elite," they can unintentionally limit your ability to analyze real-world events critically.

Destruction of Energy Infrastructure: A global wave of sabotage is observed in at least 45 oil refineries, which benefits the energy blockade and dependence strategies of nations seeking alternatives to the dollar.
- The war in Iran is not an isolated event, but part of a deliberate strategy by the United States to control global trade flows and maintain economic supremacy:
- Control of Strategic Points: The American strategy is based on controlling "choke points," such as the Strait of Hormuz and the Strait of Malacca, to force global dependence on resources, weapons, and financing from the United States.

- The Role of China and Japan: The objective would be to contain China through an indirect embargo, militarizing the "first island chain" and promoting the rearmament of Japan to create a balance of power that forces countries in the region to depend on American security.
 Internal Transformation of the US: The plan requires 3 internal pillars: a national military enlistment, continuity of the agenda (Trump's third term), and the use of an Artificial Intelligence surveillance infrastructure to prevent domestic revolts.
Conclusion: Professor Jiang concludes that the conflict will not end soon, predicting that competition between Russia and the US will intensify, while China will try to balance its position among the powers in an increasingly unstable world.

Why are refineries catching fire worldwide?

In the last 45 days, there have been *at least 45 refinery fire incidents around the world, affecting countries such as Russia, Australia, Myanmar, India, and Romania.* The exact cause is unknown and may vary between accidents or deliberate sabotage. However, applying the logic of 'means, motive, and opportunity' to analyze who benefits from the reduction in the global energy supply suggests that the actions may be being carried out by Russia or the United States to manipulate energy dependence.

How does the naval blockade affect the Chinese economy?
 The American strategy of controlling strategic bottlenecks aims to force economic dependence on China. The Strait of Malacca is the most critical point, through which 80% of the energy necessary for the functioning of the Chinese economy passes. By militarizing the 'first island chain' and establishing partnerships with nations like Indonesia and Malaysia, the US creates conditions for an indirect embargo. By restricting Chinese access to energy routes in the Middle East—a region already facing conflicts that disrupt the flow of oil—China is left with no alternatives, being forced to buy resources and energy directly from the United States, paying in dollars and submitting to American conditions. The objective is not to destroy China, but rather to economically strangle it so that the country is forced to submit to the hegemony of the dollar and the American power strategy.

### **@Monica77-q2c**
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Ts-AA6LQf6I&lc=UgymQc3Pkq1lznwOTBB4AaABAg)

Thought Experiment: The Sovereign Gambit – Is the Dollar’s "Collapse" a Controlled Demolition?

In the current geopolitical landscape of 2026, a provocative theory is gaining traction: the weakening of the U.S. Dollar is not a sign of an empire in decline, but a deliberate strategic maneuver—a "Sovereign Gambit" executed by Western elites to reset the global order.

1. The Fact-Based Reality

To understand the theory, one must look at the data. As of April 2026:

The Decline: The USD has dropped approximately 10-12% against major currencies in the last year. While dramatic, it remains the dominant reserve currency, held in nearly 60% of global reserves.

The Debt Engine: U.S. national debt has reached $39 trillion. Mathematically, this debt cannot be paid back in "expensive" dollars without crushing the economy.

The Historical Parallel: This mirrors the 1971 "Nixon Shock," when the U.S. unilaterally ended the gold standard. At the time, U.S. Treasury Secretary John Connally famously told the world: "The dollar is our currency, but it’s your problem."

2. The Strategy of "Weaponized Volatility"

The experiment suggests that by provoking a fall in the dollar, the U.S. is effectively conducting a global debt jubilee for itself while taxing its creditors. When the dollar loses value, the real value of the debt owed to China, Japan, and the EU shrinks.

Historical Likeness: This is reminiscent of the Roman Empire’s currency debasement or the post-WWII British strategy, where inflation was used to erode massive war debts, prioritizing state survival over the purchasing power of the citizenry.

3. Europe: The Silent Junior Partner

The most controversial part of this experiment is the role of the European elite. Rather than being victims of American "Greenback" volatility, European leadership may be silent architects.

Fact: Despite high energy costs and industrial flight to the U.S., EU leaders have remained remarkably aligned with Washington’s core strategic goals.

The Logic: If the Western elite perceives the rise of the BRICS nations as an existential threat to their power, they may have agreed to a "Fortress West" policy. This involves devaluing both the Dollar and the Euro to break global supply chains dependent on the East, effectively "starving" the competition of capital while re-shoring critical technology to the Transatlantic core.

4. The "Controlled Chaos" Conclusion

The theory posits that the domestic suffering—inflation, housing crises, and social unrest—is a price the elite is willing to pay.

The Endgame: Once the old, dollar-dependent global system is sufficiently destabilized, a new Transatlantic Digital Currency (CBDC) could be introduced. This would be presented as the only "safe" solution to the chaos, cementing Western control over global transactions for the next century.

Final Argument:
In this light, the dollar's "collapse" is not a failure of policy, but the ultimate exercise of power. By burning the old bridge of global trade, the West ensures that its rivals have no path forward, while it retreats into a technologically superior, albeit more authoritarian, economic fortress. It is a gamble that sacrifices the present wealth of the masses to secure the future dominance of the Empire.

### **@EconoBite**
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Ts-AA6LQf6I&lc=Ugyvpz_4v0vX5kf7T6t4AaABAg)

I really liked the video, it was great. One thing to think about when it comes to game theory is that international politics is not just about one person making a plan. It's more like a game where many people are playing together, and they don't always know what the others are doing. This makes it harder to predict what will happen. It's like a big team effort, but with many teams and not everyone has all the information.

Much of what appears to be long-term planning could very well result from players responding to each other's actions and thus developing into a feedback process of shifting equilibria.

No disagreement here – just that presenting it as an interplay between strategies as opposed to one single master strategy could perhaps make for an even better discussion point.

### **@FullLengthClassics**
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Ts-AA6LQf6I&lc=UgzIv0LaBtQS3vEA0cx4AaABAg)

Another great video. For those who do not have an hour to watch, here is a synopsis: 

The core argument is that the ongoing US-Iran war is not simply a Trump whim, but part of a deliberate long-term American imperial strategy outlined in the January 2026 National Defense Strategy document.

The Strategic Logic
The US aims to maintain its empire by controlling global trade choke points — the Strait of Hormuz, the Strait of Malacca, the Panama Canal, and others. By keeping key regions (Middle East, Europe/Russia, East Asia) in constant conflict, the US positions itself as the world's indispensable supplier of energy, weapons, and financing, all denominated in US dollars.

Why Iran Specifically
Iran sits at the center of both Russia's North-South trade corridor and China's Belt and Road Initiative. Keeping Iran destabilized cuts off both Russia and China from critical trade routes, forcing them to depend on America.

Containing China
The strategy involves militarizing the "first island chain" (Japan, Taiwan, Philippines), controlling the Strait of Malacca via Indonesia, and locking China out of the Western Hemisphere — essentially strangling China economically until it has no choice but to trade on American terms.

Domestic Preparations for Total War
The professor points to several indicators the US is preparing for prolonged conflict: automatic draft registration for 18–26 year olds, a Pentagon budget jumping from $1T to $1.5T, the Defense Production Act mobilizing Ford and GM for weapons manufacturing, and a massive expansion of ICE — which he argues is really about normalizing a domestic security apparatus, not just immigration enforcement.

Why It Will Ultimately Fail
Despite the plan's internal logic, the professor argues it will backfire due to three forces: nationalism (occupied/controlled nations eventually resist), corruption (military spending gets siphoned off), and division (domestic political polarization undermines war efforts, as happened in Vietnam).

The Bottom Line
America doesn't need to win in Iran — it just needs to keep the region in chaos. The goal is control of choke points, not conquest of territory. In the short term the US may appear dominant, but the professor predicts America will eventually be forced to retreat to the Western Hemisphere as its empire contracts.

What I appreciate most about Jiang's lectures is the preparedness for the oncoming propaganda onslaught, which i fear as with all of human history; once wise and thoughtful men will be excited and rallied unto war. I fear most that I'll be an outlier during this potential future and will face ostracization after their undefended minds are breached with pride and hubris. It also doesn't help that many of my friends have already served or are serving as a means to funding their futures. Those particular individuals seem unaware and excited at the prospect of war with Iran despite the countless examples of ANTI-WAR rhetoric since WW1. (that they've all consumed through books and movies) They seem to view much of this rhetoric as a form of fantasy and often choose to cling to a certain romanticism in war. What makes me most anxious and paranoid is trying in my own vanity to enlighten and keep them aware of the potentiality. To this I'm often met with eyes more focused on their beers. One aspect touched on by the first mainstream anti-war propaganda in 'All Quiet On The Western Front' is the fact that middle aged men on both sides of the conflict seemed resolute on the war and saw the sacrifice of their sons almost as a duty to God. Many modern middle aged men seem to have a faith in Trump that demoralizes even me, an ardent debater who often sees beyond feelings for the logic of truth; I myself was a Trump supporter once. When the 45-60 year old generation of Dad's and grandfathers whole hardily support the ferryman as he leads us toward the shore of death, It seems only inevitable their sons are bolstered and coerced. Perhaps this generation is wiser than the same ole' story of generations before and will outright refuse the war horn as Jiang somewhat predicts; and by refuse, I mean mass draft-dodging. Which I'm personally skeptical of. The rebellious personification of young American men is long overrated and I believe my piers will just as easily get in line whilst they'd simultaneously agreeing with even the most fluent and logical anti-war argument. Covid was a strong proof of this; I was living in downtown Chicago and saw, before my own eyes, the streets emptied in a matter of days. Instantly I was skeptical and did my diligence to discover simple truths that were blatantly censored like the useless of masks. I have a deviated septum and was naturally averse to denying my nostrils of even a small amount of oxygen and even refused even after flight attendants pestered me repeatedly. Despite it being documented early and made public within the first week of the Covid scare, people were still told to believe the masks must be worn. Despite this, the young fighting-aged men I referred to previously gave me stares of embarrassment and words of protest for not following this useless protocol. There were many aspects about Covid that produced reprehensible overreach by the government,  like the vaccine, but this one really stood out to me as the most demoralizing. It was like whiplash experiencing how quickly the masses followed the rules set by bureaucracy. It was a low ball opportunity to protest the very government Americans overwhelmingly grew to mistrust during the 2016 elections, yet the entire country was emotionally manipulated into defying their basic human rights of expression and oxygen. You're telling me if the situation of a draft arises, governments send letters to fathers and sons calling for their duty, and the nation refuses? I've just recently seen an article claiming Zelensky is seeking to replace Ukraine's workforce with a new influx of African Migrants after their native men were all manipulated and forcibly exiled to their deaths. One theory Jiang espouses is America will utilize Central/South American migrants to fight the war by dangling passports, which is perhaps more immoral than manipulating native populations, but as any thinking head knows, the only threat to oligarchical tyranny is the white man. (DEI, women being favored for positions of authority, media demoralization) I think the last 8 years have all been arranged to ensure the erasure of the same demographics as Ukraine's. Jiang once said he has great admiration for America and its founding mythos of meritocracy and claimed he was accepted by a group of aged white Americans because of his message, even defying their immediate and natural racial bias'. Jiang knows as well as anyone who sees beyond the veil of propaganda that America was a true meritocracy.  My devout Trump supporting Uncle lampooned me with the observation that today's white men are pathetic. He even went so far as to claim they deserve to be replaced with more Indian immigrants, who in his eyes simply work harder and are always innovating. What he fails to understand is that intelligent competitive men don't play games they don't *believe* they can win. The entire western apparatus has been damn-near designed to reward stagnation and punish competency, whether through evil conniving or the natural means of modernity. In either sense It's destroyed our sense of pride while simultaneously imbuing shame, leaving the grand scale depressed and resentful. 45% of college graduates are unemployed, and Trump's lackey's and the regimes puppets continue to claim we don't have enough working men. ### **@barryrobbins7694**
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Ts-AA6LQf6I&lc=UgyRMMqQEdb_c0OpQqt4AaABAg)

“The war, therefore if we judge it by the standards of previous wars, is merely an imposture. It is like the battles between certain ruminant animals whose horns are incapable of hurting one another. But though it is unreal it is not meaningless. It eats up the surplus of consumable goods, and it helps to preserve the special mental atmosphere that the hierarchical society needs. War, it will be seen, is now a purely internal affair. In the past, the ruling groups of all countries, although they might recognize their common interest and therefore limit the destructiveness of war, did fight against one another, and the victor always plundered the vanquished. In our own day they are not fighting against one another at all. The war is waged by each ruling group against its own subjects, and the object of the war is not to make or prevent conquests of territory, but to keep the structure of society intact. The very word "war," therefore, has become misleading. It would probably be accurate to say that by becoming continuous war has ceased to exist. The peculiar pressure that is exerted on human beings between the Neolithic Age and the early twentieth century has disappeared and has been replaced by something quite different. The effect would be much the same if the three superstates, instead of fighting one another, should agree to live in perpetual peace, each inviolate within its own boundaries. For in that case each would still be a self-contained universe, freed forever from the sobering influence of external danger. A peace that was truly permanent would be the same as a permanent war. This--although the vast majority of Party members understand it only in a shallower sense--is the inner meaning of the Party slogan: WAR IS PEACE.”

George Orwell, 1984
